    """

    Imagine a road that's a perfectly straight infinitely long line.

    are driving right to left.   The two sets of cars start out being very far from

    when a car that's moving left to right hits a car that's moving right to left.

    in their trajectory as if they did not collide.

    This function outputs the number of such collisions.
    """

def fibfib(n: int):
    """The FibFib number sequence is a sequence similar to the Fibbonacci sequnece that's defined as follows:
    fibfib(0) == 0
    fibfib(1) == 0
    fibfib(2) == 1
    fibfib(n) == fibfib(n-1) + fibfib(n-2) + fibfib(n-3).
    Please write a function to efficiently compute the n-th element of the fibfib number sequence.
    >>> fibfib(1)
    0
    >>> fibfib(5)
    4
    >>> fibfib(8)
    24
    """

def fruit_distribution(s,n):
    """
    In this task, you will be given a string that represents a number of apples and oranges 
    that are distributed in a basket of fruit this basket contains 
    apples, oranges, and mango fruits. Given the string that represents the total number of 
    the oranges and apples and an integer that represent the total number of the fruits 
    in the basket return the number of the mango fruits in the basket.
    for examble:
    fruit_distribution("5 apples and 6 oranges", 19) ->19 - 5 - 6 = 8
    fruit_distribution("0 apples and 1 oranges",3) -> 3 - 0 - 1 = 2
    fruit_distribution("2 apples and 3 oranges", 100) -> 100 - 2 - 3 = 95
    fruit_distribution("100 apples and 1 oranges",120) -> 120 - 100 - 1 = 19
    """

def pluck(arr):
    """
    "Given an array representing a branch of a tree that has non-negative integer nodes
    your task is to pluck one of the nodes and return it.
    The plucked node should be the node with the smallest even value.
    If multiple nodes with the same smallest even value are found return the node that has smallest index.

    The plucked node should be returned in a list, [ smalest_value, its index ],
    If there are no even values or the given array is empty, return [].

    Example 1:
        Input: [4,2,3]
        Output: [2, 1]
        Explanation: 2 has the smallest even value, and 2 has the smallest index.

    Example 2:
        Input: [1,2,3]
        Output: [2, 1]
        Explanation: 2 has the smallest even value, and 2 has the smallest index. 

    Example 3:
        Input: []
        Output: []
    
    Example 4:
        Input: [5, 0, 3, 0, 4, 2]
        Output: [0, 1]
        Explanation: 0 is the smallest value, but  there are two zeros,
                     so we will choose the first zero, which has the smallest index.

    Constraints:
        * 1 <= nodes.length <= 10000
        * 0 <= node.value
    """

def sort_array(arr):
    """
    In this Kata, you have to sort an array of non-negative integers according to
    number of ones in their binary representation in ascending order.
    For similar number of ones, sort based on decimal value.

    It must be implemented like this:
    >>> sort_array([1, 5, 2, 3, 4]) == [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]
    >>> sort_array([-2, -3, -4, -5, -6]) == [-6, -5, -4, -3, -2]

def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
```python
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list of odd numbers in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture involves a sequence starting with any positive integer n:
    - If the term is even, the next term is half of the current term.
    - If the term is odd, the next term is 3 times the current term plus 1.
    The sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. The returned list is sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], odd numbers are 1 and 5.
    """
```

    Note: The given list is guaranteed to have unique elements.

    move_one_ball([3, 4, 5, 1, 2])==>True
    Explanation: By performin 2 right shift operations, non-decreasing order can
                 be achieved for the given array.
    move_one_ball([3, 5, 4, 1, 2])==>False
    Explanation:It is not possible to get non-decreasing order for the given

    to the right. The element at the last position will be relocated to the beginning 
    of the array, i.e., the 0th index.

    return True; otherwise, return False. In the case of an empty array, return True.

    Note: The provided list will have unique elements.

def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
```python
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, produce a sorted list containing the odd numbers found in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a proposal in mathematics concerning a sequence defined in this way: begin with any positive integer n. Each subsequent term is derived from the preceding one as follows: if the preceding term is even, the following term is half of the preceding term. If the preceding term is odd, the next term is three times the preceding term plus one. The conjecture states that regardless of the initial value of n, the sequence will eventually arrive at 1.

    Note:
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. The resulting list is sorted in ascending order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The Collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are 1 and 5.
    """
```

      arr = []

      arr = [1, -1 , 55]

def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, produce a sorted list of the odd numbers found in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a mathematical idea involving a sequence which is generated as follows: begin with any positive integer n. Each term is then derived from the one before it as follows: if the preceding term is even, divide it by two to get the next term. If the preceding term is odd, the next term is calculated by tripling the previous term and adding one. The conjecture states that regardless of the starting number n, the sequence will inevitably reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) equals [1].
        2. The list returned should be sorted in ascending order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The Collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

    288

    The function will receive an integer as input and should return the special
    factorial of this integer.
    """

def special_factorial(n):
    """The Brazilian factorial is calculated by multiplying the factorials of all numbers from n down to 1:
    brazilian_factorial(n) = n! * (n-1)! * (n-2)! * ... * 1!
    where n > 0

    For example:
    >>> special_factorial(4)
    288

def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
```python
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, produce a sorted list containing the odd numbers found in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is an unsolved proposition in mathematics that describes a sequence formulated
    as follows: starting with any positive integer n. Each subsequent term is determined from the 
    preceding term as such: if the preceding term is even, the subsequent term is half of the 
    preceding term. If it is odd, the subsequent term is 3 times the preceding term plus 1. The conjecture claims
    that no matter which value of n you start with, the sequence will eventually reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) results in [1].
        2. The list returned is sorted in ascending order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """
```

def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:

    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note:

        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    """You have to write a function which validates a given date string and
    returns True if the date is valid otherwise False.
    The date is valid if all of the following rules are satisfied:
    1. The date string is not empty.
    2. The number of days is not less than 1 or higher than 31 days for months 1,3,5,7,8,10,12. And the number of days is not less than 1 or higher than 30 days for months 4,6,9,11. And, the number of days is not less than 1 or higher than 29 for the month 2.

def by_length(arr):
    """
    Given an array of integers, sort the integers that are between 1 and 9 inclusive,
    reverse the resulting array, and then replace each digit by its corresponding name from
    "One", "Two", "Three", "Four", "Five", "Six", "Seven", "Eight", "Nine".

    For example:
      arr = [2, 1, 1, 4, 5, 8, 2, 3]   
            -> sort arr -> [1, 1, 2, 2, 3, 4, 5, 8] 
            -> reverse arr -> [8, 5, 4, 3, 2, 2, 1, 1]
      return ["Eight", "Five", "Four", "Three", "Two", "Two", "One", "One"]
    
      If the array is empty, return an empty array:
      arr = []
      return []
    
      If the array has any strange number ignore it:
      arr = [1, -1 , 55] 
            -> sort arr -> [-1, 1, 55]
            -> reverse arr -> [55, 1, -1]
      return = ['One']
    """

def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
```python
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list of odd numbers in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture involves a sequence where:
    - Start with any positive integer n.
    - If the term is even, the next term is half of it.
    - If the term is odd, the next term is 3 times it plus 1.
    The sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. Return the list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are 1 and 5.
    """
```

def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:

```python
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list with odd numbers in the Collatz sequence.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The Collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], with odd numbers 1 and 5.
    """
```

def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.
        3. Only distinct odd numbers should be included in the result list.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list containing the odd numbers in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a hypothesis in mathematics that deals with a sequence generated
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Each subsequent term is derived from the
    preceding term in this manner: if the preceding term is even, the following term is half
    of the preceding term. If the preceding term is odd, the following term is three times the preceding
    term plus one. The conjecture posits that regardless of the starting value of n, the sequence will invariably reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. The returned list is arranged in ascending order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The Collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], hence the only odd numbers are 1 and 5.
    """

def compare(game,guess):
    """I think we all remember that feeling when the result of some long-awaited
    event is finally known. The feelings and thoughts you have at that moment are
    definitely worth noting down and comparing.
    Your task is to determine if a person correctly guessed the results of a number of matches.
    You are given two arrays of scores and guesses of equal length, where each index shows a match. 
    Return an array of the same length denoting how far off each guess was. If they have guessed correctly,
    the value is 0, and if not, the value is the absolute difference between the guess and the score.

 def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list containing the odd numbers in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a hypothesis in mathematics that deals with a sequence generated
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Each subsequent term is derived from the
    preceding term in this manner: if the preceding term is even, the following term is half
    of the preceding term. If the preceding term is odd, the following term is three times the preceding
    term plus one. The conjecture posits that regardless of the starting value of n, the sequence will invariably reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. The returned list is arranged in ascending order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The Collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], hence the only odd numbers are 1 and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that contains the odd numbers found in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a mathematical hypothesis concerning a sequence created as follows:
    begin with any positive integer n. Each next term is calculated based on the previous one
    in this way: if the previous term is even, the next term is its half. If the previous term is odd,
    the next term is three times the previous term plus one. The conjecture suggests that no matter 
    the starting value of n, the sequence will eventually arrive at 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. The returned list is sorted in ascending order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The Collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], hence the only odd numbers are 1 and 5.
    """

 def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list containing the odd numbers in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a hypothesis in mathematics that deals with a sequence generated
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Each subsequent term is derived from the
    preceding term in this manner: if the preceding term is even, the following term is half
    of the preceding term. If the preceding term is odd, the following term is three times the preceding
    term plus one. The conjecture posits that regardless of the starting value of n, the sequence will invariably reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. The returned list is arranged in ascending order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The Collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], hence the only odd numbers are 1 and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list containing the odd numbers in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz sequence starts with a positive integer n. If the term is even, the next term is half of it. If odd, the next term is three times the term plus one. The sequence will reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. The returned list is in ascending order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The Collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], hence the only odd numbers are 1 and 5.
    """

seed prompt:
 def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list containing the odd numbers in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a hypothesis in mathematics that deals with a sequence generated
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Each subsequent term is derived from the
    preceding term in this manner: if the preceding term is even, the following term is half
    of the preceding term. If the preceding term is odd, the following term is three times the preceding
    term plus one. The conjecture posits that regardless of the starting value of n, the sequence will invariably reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. The returned list is arranged in ascending order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The Collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], hence the only odd numbers are 1 and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list containing the odd numbers in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a hypothesis in mathematics that deals with a sequence generated
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Each subsequent term is derived from the
    preceding term in this manner: if the preceding term is even, the following term is half
    of the preceding term. If the preceding term is odd, the following term is three times the preceding
    term plus one. The conjecture posits that regardless of the starting value of n, the sequence will invariably reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. The returned list is arranged in ascending order.
    
    Ensure that the function handles large inputs efficiently.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The Collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], hence the only odd numbers are 1 and 5.
    """

seed prompt:
 def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list containing the odd numbers in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a hypothesis in mathematics that deals with a sequence generated
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Each subsequent term is derived from the
    preceding term in this manner: if the preceding term is even, the following term is half
    of the preceding term. If the preceding term is odd, the following term is three times the preceding
    term plus one. The conjecture posits that regardless of the starting value of n, the sequence will invariably reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. The returned list is arranged in ascending order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The Collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], hence the only odd numbers are 1 and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Provided a positive integer n, retrieve a list of the odd numbers in the Collatz sequence, sorted in ascending order.

    The Collatz conjecture proposes a mathematical sequence generated by the following process: begin with any positive integer n. Each subsequent term is established from the previous term in this way: if the previous term is even, the next term will be half of it. If the previous term is odd, the next term will be calculated as three times the previous term plus one. The conjecture asserts that no matter the starting value of n, the sequence will eventually reach 1.

    Important Notes: 
        1. Collatz(1) results in [1].
        2. The list returned should be sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The Collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], hence the only odd numbers are 1 and 5.
    """
